BRL-CAD Obol Migration Worklog
==============================

How To Use This File
--------------------

This file preserves the detailed Obol migration work history, design notes,
and rationale consolidated from the former root guidance documents.  It is a
reference log, not the first-stop active handoff.

Use obol_activework.txt first for current priorities, completion percentages,
test gates, and remaining work.  Return here when an implementation choice
needs historical context or more detailed design rationale.

Current Work Notes
------------------

The active handoff documentation has been compacted.  `obol_activework.txt`
now keeps only the current migration snapshot, remaining direct legacy-view
counts, active queue, verification gates, and done criteria; detailed slice
history remains here in `obol_worklog.txt`.  This restores activework to its
intended role as a short first-read file instead of a growing historical
archive.

The pivot guard now has a central production allowlist for direct
`rt/view_legacy_bsg.h` includes.  The detailed boundary-specific CMake checks
remain in place, but the direct legacy-view owner count now has one obvious
list to update as MGED/TclCAD/libdm/qtcad/librt files migrate.  A compiled
O3 scanner remains a reasonable future optimization for repetitive
file/token checks if the CMake guard becomes a bottleneck; it was not added
in this pass because the guard still completes comfortably under the
180-second cap and the current script contains many semantic checks that are
not worth reimplementing prematurely.

Current status reporting now calls out libbsg retirement readiness explicitly:
the migration is not retire-ready while the 29 direct production
`rt/view_legacy_bsg.h` owners remain, with Phase 4 focused on the 6 MGED and
13 TclCAD owners before Phase 5 lower display/backend and bridge cleanup.

The MGED setup/attach lifecycle boundary now uses public GED view-context
facades instead of direct `rt/view_legacy_bsg.h` access.  `src/mged/setup.c`
creates and registers the initial view with `ged_view_context_create_with_set`
and `ged_view_set_context_add`, installs `mged_view_callback` through
`ged_view_context_update_callback_set`, and reads the initial center through
`ged_view_context_center_get`.  `src/mged/attach.c` now uses GED facades for
ADC state writes, DM scale-storage handoff, copied-view creation/update
callback setup, and copied-display LoD policy adjustment.  The public
`include/ged/view.h` surface now has opaque create-copy and update-callback
facades, with the raw copy allocation contained in the RT legacy adapter.

The pivot guard now treats `setup.c` and `attach.c` as migrated facade
consumers: it requires the new GED lifecycle/callback/LoD/ADC/scale-storage
tokens and rejects direct legacy-header inclusion, local `struct bsg_view`
allocation, direct RT legacy lifecycle/callback/LoD/ADC/scale-storage calls,
and raw BSG view-center access from returning.  Direct non-test
`rt/view_legacy_bsg.h` users are now down to 6 in `src/mged`, 13 in
`src/libtclcad`, and 29 across the broader production source scan.

Verification on FreeBSD/clang passed with:
`ctest --test-dir build -R "^brlobol_pivot_guard$" --timeout 180 --output-on-failure`
completing 1/1 passing in 29.62 seconds, `ninja -C build bin/mged`
completing successfully, and focused guard/hygiene/MGED-view/drawing
verification with
`ctest --test-dir build -R "^(brlobol_pivot_guard|libbsg_api_hygiene|rt_view_legacy_bsg|ged_test_(view_command|state_services|drawing_basic|drawing_quad|mged_bsg|ged_draw_scene|qged_swrast|view_independent)|test_qtcad_obol_draw_sync|regress-(mged_(adc|overlay|view|draw|refresh|draw_e_erase_d_who|setview|rot_view|sca_view|tra_view|size|zoom|knob)|nirt|gchecker|gdiff))$" -E "^slow-" --timeout 180 --output-on-failure`
completing 28/28 passing in 186.65 seconds.  The longest member was
`ged_test_state_services` at 93.48 seconds, followed by
`libbsg_api_hygiene` at 40.91 seconds, `brlobol_pivot_guard` at
14.84 seconds, `ged_test_drawing_quad` at 12.71 seconds, and
`regress-gchecker` at 8.32 seconds.  The previously used extra MGED ARB
regression was also checked separately with
`ctest --test-dir build -R "^regress-mged_arb$" --timeout 180 --output-on-failure`,
completing 1/1 passing in 0.46 seconds real time with the test itself at
0.41 seconds.

A one-hundred-forty-sixth MGED display-manager event facade cleanup moved the
remaining `src/mged/dm-generic.c` retained-view reads and simple state writes
behind public GED view-context facades.  `include/ged/view.h` now exposes
`ged_view_context_refresh_request`, `ged_view_context_zclip_set`,
`ged_view_context_lod_policy_get`, and
`ged_view_context_lod_policy_apply`, with `src/libged/ged.cpp` keeping the
temporary delegation to the retained RT legacy adapter inside the GED-owned
boundary.  `dm-generic.c` now uses GED facades for zoom-refresh LoD policy
inspection, mouse/ADC center, scale, model-to-view, and view-to-model reads,
display-setting refresh requests, zclip sync, and display-manager handoff.

`dm-generic.c` dropped `rt/view_legacy_bsg.h`.  The pivot guard now requires
the GED facade tokens in this MGED boundary, splits `dm-generic.c` out of the
older legacy display-manager and refresh classifications, and rejects stale
`rt/view_legacy_bsg.h`, direct retained view-field reads/writes, and direct
RT legacy adapter calls from returning there.  Direct non-test
`rt/view_legacy_bsg.h` users are now down to 8 in `src/mged`, 13 in
`src/libtclcad`, and 31 across the broader production source scan.

A one-hundred-forty-first libged display-manager command facade cleanup added
public GED view-context facades for retained view creation with an existing
view set, view-set context registration, display-manager set, width/height
reads, dimension writes, and scale-storage handoff.  `include/ged/view.h` now
exposes `ged_view_context_create_with_set`, `ged_view_set_context_add`,
`ged_view_context_display_manager_set`, `ged_view_context_width_get`,
`ged_view_context_height_get`, `ged_view_context_dimensions_set`, and
`ged_view_context_scale_storage_get`; `src/libged/ged.cpp` keeps the temporary
delegation to the retained RT legacy adapter inside the GED-owned boundary.

`src/libged/dm/dm.c` now uses GED facades for DM name lookup, current-DM
resolution, DM list enumeration, attach-time duplicate-view diagnostics,
attach-view creation and registration, fallback dimensions, scale-storage
handoff, and display-manager binding.  The file no longer includes
`rt/view_legacy_bsg.h`, no longer calls direct RT legacy view-context
adapters, and no longer owns local typed `struct bsg_view` allocation for
attach-view creation.  Production `src/libged` direct legacy-header ownership
is now confined to the bridge-owned `src/libged/ged.cpp` and
`src/libged/bsg_ged_draw_view.c` files.

The pivot guard now requires the GED display-manager, view-name,
create/register, dimension, and scale-storage facade tokens in `dm.c`, rejects
stale `rt/view_legacy_bsg.h` and direct RT legacy adapter calls from returning
there, and preserves the remaining bridge-owned RT legacy adapter requirements
inside `ged.cpp`.

A one-hundred-fortieth libged ADC/knob facade cleanup added public GED
view-context facades for retained ADC state get/set and view knob value
snapshots, rate reset, calibration, command processing, translation,
rotation, and rate-flag updates.  `include/ged/view.h` now exposes
`ged_view_context_adc_state_get`, `ged_view_context_adc_state_set`,
`ged_view_context_knob_values_get`, `ged_view_context_knobs_reset`,

boundary.

`src/libged/adc/adc.c` now uses GED scale, model-to-view, view-to-model, and
ADC-state facades while keeping ADC math on neutral `rt/view.h` helpers.
`src/libged/view/knob.c` now uses GED facades for knob-value snapshots,
unit-conversion sync, coordinate defaults, rate reset, calibration, knob
command processing, translation, keypoint-origin lookup, rotation, and
rate-flag updates, and uses the neutral `RT_VIEW_KNOBS_RATE` category.  Both
files dropped `rt/view_legacy_bsg.h`.  The neutral
`rt_view_knob_values` snapshot moved from `include/rt/view_legacy_bsg.h` to
`include/rt/view.h`.

The pivot guard now requires the GED ADC and knob facade tokens in the
migrated command files, rejects stale `rt/view_legacy_bsg.h` includes there,
and rejects direct RT legacy ADC, matrix, knob, unit-conversion, and
BSG-suffixed knob-category calls from returning to those command boundaries.

`_dm_cmd_attach` carry active and named views as `void *`; and the attach
fallback path uses `rt_view_context_width_from_bsg`,

`rt_view_context_dimensions_set_bsg`,
`rt_view_context_scale_storage_from_bsg`,
`rt_view_context_display_manager_set_bsg`,
`rt_view_context_init_bsg`, and `rt_view_set_context_add_bsg`.

The only remaining local `struct bsg_view` use in `dm.c` is the transitional
owned allocation for a freshly created attach view, which remains registered
with GED's existing free-view table.  The broad libged typed-view read scan is
now down to 9 hits: six in the intentional `bsg_ged_draw_view.c` bridge, plus
the larger `draw.c`, `bigE.c`, and `view.c` command/shared-struct cleanups.

scale-storage, lifecycle-init, and view-set-add helper tokens in `dm.c`; it
rejects typed active-view casts, typed display-manager calls, typed

from returning to this display-manager command batch.

direct libbsg links from selected converter and simulation targets whose
sources do not use BSG APIs directly.  The Unigraphics `ug-g`, Cubit
`g-sat`, and Creo `creo-brl` direct shim/package rows now list only their

migration work.

This is intentionally a build-boundary cleanup rather than a source migration:
the source trees already had no direct `bsg_*`, `BSG_*`, or `bsg/` usage, so
the direct libbsg rows were preserving a noisier dependency graph than the
code required.  The pivot guard now checks the selected CMake files for stale
libbsg relinks and scans the corresponding source trees to make sure direct
BSG API use does not appear without an explicit migration decision.

Original Obol Pivot Migration Plan
----------------------------------

Purpose
-------

BRL-CAD should use Obol as the canonical scene, view, interaction, and
rendering API for interactive 3D applications.

The migration is parallel-first: build the Obol path beside the current
BSG/DM path until it reaches production parity, then remove public libbsg,
libdm, dm_plugins, and BSG/DM-shaped application APIs.  Temporary internal
bridges are allowed while a phase is incomplete.  No long-lived public BSG
or DM compatibility layer is part of the target design.

libbrlobol is the BRL-CAD-specific Obol extension library.  It should define
BRL-CAD Obol nodes, actions, details, and narrow application services.  It
must not become a generic replacement for BSG's render-item/backend-scene
model.

The first production target is qged/qtcad.  After qged is usable on Obol,
migrate GED/TclCAD/mged, then remove lower-level BSG/DM utility
dependencies.


Guiding Decisions
-----------------

* Obol cameras, transforms, SoViewport, SoRenderManager, actions, nodes,
  fields, and details are authoritative for scene, view, render, and
  interaction state.
* New drawing code should use Obol directly or use BRL-CAD Obol extensions.
  It should not add new BSG/DM dependencies outside explicit legacy
  allowlists.
* BRL-CAD-specific policy belongs in libbrlobol.  Generic scene graph,
  traversal, rendering, text, picking, and backend features should be exposed
  through Obol rather than rewrapped as parallel BSG concepts.
* C++ is acceptable for code that directly interacts with the drawing API.
  C and Tcl callers may use opaque bridges backed by Obol.
* bg_vlist and bg_line_layer are low-level geometry/debug containers, not
  scene APIs.  Application-visible drawing state should be Obol nodes.
* UNIX plot3 and TIG-style helpers are legacy plot/export/diagnostic support.
  They should not become Obol scene concepts.
* bg_line_layer should stay independent from plot3.  It is an in-memory
  diagnostic/overlay container; plot3 is a legacy file-stream format and
  helper API.  Conversion helpers between bg_line_layer, plot3 output, and
  Obol overlay nodes are appropriate, but moving line_layer into plot3.h
  would couple normal diagnostic geometry to a legacy export format.
* Scene text, labels, HUD text, and annotations should use Obol 2D/3D text.
  If flattened text geometry is needed, use an Obol-backed text-to-geometry
  conversion/export path rather than preserving vectfont as a scene API.
* Obol's pluggable backend model is the intended replacement for libdm's
  rendering/display role.
* libimgstream is the target home for live mutable image streams, async image
  producers, dirty-update notification, transport, and framebuffer protocol
  compatibility.  It is not a display backend system.
* libicv remains the target home for static image containers, codecs, image
  file I/O, image transforms, and neutral lookup-table/colormap operations.
* Obol owns image display, placement, layering, texture upload, readback, and
  rendering backend selection.  Framebuffer pan/zoom/cursor/overlay behavior
  is display compatibility state realized through Obol/application display
  hosts, not core image-stream storage.
* fb compatibility belongs inside libimgstream as a clearly scoped
  compatibility layer while callers migrate.  It should preserve useful
  host:port, ipc:<addr>, PKG_ADDR, FBSERV_TOKEN, FBSERV_TLS, and MSG_FB*
  workflows without preserving libdm's framebuffer backend abstraction.


Current State
-------------

libbrlobol and qtcad:

* brlobol_init registers BRL-CAD Obol classes and supports replacing the
  active Obol context manager after initial setup.
* BRLObolViewController owns the application scene root, active camera,
  SoViewport, SoRenderManager, viewport region, and render-request lifecycle.
* qtcad canvases expose the Obol controller and an Obol viewport image
  readback path.
* qtcad installs an application-owned Obol context manager backed by Qt
  offscreen GL, with OSMesa fallback when Qt offscreen creation is not
  available.
* qtcad GL/SW viewport capture uses Obol readback directly when migrated Obol
  scene content exists, consumes the Obol render request, and does not
  initialize the legacy display manager for that content.  SW visible paint
  follows the same direct Obol readback path.  GL migrated-content paint now
  renders through BRLObolViewController::renderPending with the Qt GL context
  current before legacy DM initialization, and falls back to legacy DM drawing
  only when Obol rendering is not available.
* qtcad default keyboard/mouse/wheel navigation no longer requires a legacy
  display manager before it can update the transitional view and synchronize
  Obol camera/render state.
* qtcad point, rectangle, and ray selection filters prefer Obol pick identity
  for migrated content and do not initialize the legacy display manager when
  Obol supplies the selection paths.  The public qtcad selection filter API
  now exposes normalized paths instead of BSG pick/interaction result
  accessors, and the qged selector plugin consumes those paths directly.
  The qtcad point-pick helper also has the first nonblocking source-backed
  exact mesh pick integration: it keeps ordinary display picks as the first
  pass, then delegates source-backed full-detail mesh picking to the shared
  BRLObolViewController exact-ray helper.  Rectangle picks inherit this
  through their point samples.  qtcad point, ray, and rectangle picks also use
  the controller-owned reusable librt exact-ray cache as a database-source
  fallback for implicit solids and implicit-backed combs when no ordinary
  display/source-backed mesh hit is available.  qtcad pick records now carry
  source-backed mesh face vertex and nearest edge/vertex identity plus
  edit-intent metadata for exact mesh hits.  qtcad point, rectangle, and ray
  selection filters also honor pending source-backed exact-pick submissions by
  clearing immediate selections and skipping legacy BSG fallback until the
  exact detail result is ready.  The explicit-ray selection path derives
  screen-to-view and view-radius data through the RT legacy view adapter
  boundary, and qtcad sketch plus 2D measurement screen-to-view conversions use
  the same adapter instead of calling the BSG helper directly.  Legacy BSG pick
  paths remain internal fallback.
* qtcad Obol measurement and snap workflows operate on migrated content
  without initializing the legacy display manager.  Measurement overlays are
  now published exclusively through Obol; the qtcad measurement filter no
  longer creates BSG measurement features or caches BSG measure-result
  records, and 3D measurement no longer falls back through BSG pick
  candidates or direct raytrace prep.  qtcad now exposes full-detail snap,
  export, and geometry-measure helpers that delegate source-backed LoD
  drain/submit work to BRLObolViewController while the ordinary interactive
  snap helper remains display-level.  Those exact helper records report
  source-backed triangle identity, counts, bounds, area/length, and
  nearest-primitive plus nearest face/vertex/edge identity, with edit-intent
  metadata preserved.  They also report submitted source-detail work when a
  nonblocking exact call has no result yet, giving tool callers an explicit
  retry/pending signal instead of conflating that state with a true miss.
  The controller passes its exact full-detail face/point
  budget to source-backed provider tasks so over-budget whole-object reloads
  can fall back before mesh allocation.  Snap
  retains
  transitional legacy refinement fallback for non-migrated or view-scoped
  cases.
* qtcad Obol workflow tests cover pinewood.g, havoc.g, and the larger
  mixed-primitive m35.g sample, including real-model readback, pick, snap,
  and measurement overlay helpers.  doc/notes/obol_realization_coverage.txt
  now records share/db/faa/Generic_Twin.g as the larger FAA maturity candidate
  once the large-model staged-LoD path is ready for routine gating, and
  test_qtcad_obol_real_models exposes it as an opt-in
  BRLOBOL_QTCAD_GENERIC_TWIN=1 draw/readback check.
* libbrlobol mesh face picks now preserve the picked triangle's source
  coordinate indices in SoBRLPickDetail, and SoBRLExportAction triangle
  records carry the same vertex-index identity.  Face pick details also record
  the nearest source edge endpoints, nearest source vertex, and object-space
  hit point for deterministic mesh sub-entity inspection.  SoBRLSnapAction
  can now report source-indexed mesh vertex and edge-nearest candidates, and
  SoBRLMeasureAction records nearest-face vertex/edge identity while
  preserving the existing face-nearest measurement result.  Edit-preview
  geometry now carries typed edit-intent role/id metadata through explicit
  SoBRLEditPreview fields, shape fields, pick details, snap candidates,
  measure nearest results, export records, and source-backed mesh request
  identity.  The qtcad edit-preview helper has an additive update-with-intent
  entry point so callers can keep preview-node identity separate from the
  live edit operation or target being previewed, and the helper test verifies
  controller-published custom intent metadata through snap/measure/export
  traversal.
  Full standalone edge and vertex picking for non-mesh/native curved entities
  plus broader live edit-tool lifecycle/state semantics remain queued.
* libbrlobol BoT mesh realization now copies existing RT-owned mesh LoD
  metadata onto SoBRLMeshShape fields when a neutral rt_mesh_lod cache result
  is already available.  It records active level, counts, snapped-point/normal
  flags, and bounds without exposing BSG LoD containers or forcing synchronous
  cache generation during normal Obol realization.  The bridge now expresses
  that copied metadata as a provider-neutral BRLObolLodRequest/Result and
  geometry-handle record before publishing the existing Obol fields.
  The neutral BRLObolLodMeshPayload also owns optional active RT mesh normals
  as one normal per triangle-corner index when a provider publishes them, and
  rejects mismatched normal counts before staged payload application.
  Cached POP mesh-LoD normal publication is now bounded by the loaded
  triangle-index buffer size before librt exposes it, and the brlobol RT
  mesh-LoD provider test seeds a normal-bearing cache entry to verify the
  normals survive the RT facade and neutral payload copy.  The RT
  `db_mesh_lod_store_mesh` and transitional `bsg_mesh_lod_cache` public
  comments now make the generated-store normal contract explicit: optional
  normals are one normal per triangle corner in face-index order, and the
  neutral rt_view_info test stores a normal-bearing generated mesh to verify
  active LoD data reports `normal_count == face_count * 3`.  Direct cached
  mesh-LoD views with no full-detail callback now clamp requests above the
  cached POP threshold to the highest cached POP level instead of clearing the
  POP arrays and leaving stale active mesh pointers.  The RT full-detail
  mesh-LoD callback bridge now also validates optional callback normals before
  lending full-detail arrays to the POP implementation: normals must be absent
  with a zero count or present as one normal per triangle corner, and malformed
  normal metadata leaves no active borrowed payload.  Replacing a mesh-LoD
  detail callback now invokes the previous full-detail free hook before the
  callback data pointer is overwritten, so provider-owned callback state does
  not leak when ownership changes on a live LoD handle.  A neutral
  `rt_mesh_lod_detail_callbacks_clear` helper now releases the installed
  detail callback owner without destroying the LoD handle, allowing callers to
  return a live handle to cached POP-only data.  If full-detail callback setup
  fails, the requested level load now fails, leaves the active level invalid,
  and clears previously published active mesh arrays instead of reporting a
  current full-detail level with no active data.
  Full-detail callback payloads with missing required faces, display points,
  or original-point arrays, or with face indices outside the supplied
  display-point or original-point arrays, are now rejected before publication,
  and the RT regressions cover those failures after a valid POP payload is
  active.
  The public RT mesh-LoD active/data/info helpers now use the same required
  original-point contract, so they do not report a usable active mesh unless
  faces, display points, and original points are all present.
  Producer setup callbacks that return failure after filling a partial detail
  record now have their clear hook invoked before the load fails, and the RT
  regression verifies stale active data is still cleared.
  Built-in BoT full-detail publication and cache refresh/store paths now share
  the same RT mesh-array validation as neutral callbacks, and invalid BoT
  cache generation is rejected before BSG sees malformed face indices.
  Invalid generated-mesh replacement stores now return the current cache
  status and leave an existing generated cache entry intact instead of failing
  before object status is populated.
  Replacing or clearing callback ownership while borrowed full-detail arrays
  are active now invalidates that level immediately, so same-level reloads
  cannot skip setup against freed provider storage; callback-owner changes
  made while cached POP data is active republish the POP arrays instead of
  dropping visible data.
  SoBRLExportAction triangle records now carry that same copied LoD summary
  for diagnostic export and staged-realization verification.
* libbrlobol now has the first provider-neutral BRLObolLodService skeleton
  for staged realization work.  It owns explicit start/stop, joinable worker
  threads, generation cancellation, dependency-aware task scheduling, UI-thread
  result draining, in-flight diagnostics, cancellable task-level debug delay
  controls, stale result rejection, owned realization-task data cleanup, and
  one serialized cache-write lane without exposing BSG LoD APIs.  It also has
  the coalesced result-ready wakeup described in the consolidated LoD notes:
  workers only signal that queued results should be drained, and the owning
  view/controller performs the main-thread drain/apply pass.  The LoD result
  contract now carries typed Stage 0/1/2 directory/dependency records, display
  attributes, AABB results, and proxy/OBB payloads so early placeholder states
  can be generated and inspected before final mesh payloads arrive.
  SoBRLMeshShape can now consume staged LoD results into Obol fields, reject
  stale result keys, and publish cached RT mesh results through the same
  staged-result path.  Staged mesh results can now carry an owned Obol-side
  triangle payload copied from an active RT mesh-LoD data view, and
  SoBRLMeshShape can apply that payload through its existing indexed-triangle
  fields without borrowing RT or BSG arrays.  SoBRLMeshShape can also build a
  provider-neutral BRLObolLodRequest from its Obol identity, policy, counts,
  and bounds fields, and BRLObolViewController can derive a sanitized
  rt_view_info snapshot from its Obol camera and viewport state.  The first
  SoBRLMeshLodSubmitAction can walk Obol mesh nodes, build deterministic
  requests from those fields, own RT mesh-cache provider state through the
  service task lifetime, and submit view-driven cached mesh refresh/load work
  explicitly.  BRLObolViewController now exposes a narrow controller hook that
  derives the active rt_view_info snapshot, submits per-database-source mesh
  LoD requests through that action, drains completed service results, and
  applies them back through the update action while recording counts and
  diagnostics; it can retain an active LoD service subscription, mark pending
  results from the wakeup callback without touching scene nodes, and process
  those results before rendering.  It also has the first coalesced auto-submit
  gate for changed view/policy/source signatures, so render-time preparation
  can enqueue fresh RT mesh-cache provider work without duplicate submissions
  for the same stable scene.  The first SoBRLLodUpdateAction drains LoD service
  results on the UI/main thread, matches them to SoBRLMeshShape nodes by
  source path/name, applies accepted staged fields and owned mesh payloads,
  and records rejected or unmatched result diagnostics.  The first RT
  mesh-cache provider task can refresh/load cached RT mesh LoD through the
  service, optionally force a requested RT LoD level, shrink active RT arrays
  after copying, and return the neutral staged mesh result with an owned mesh
  copy.  SoBRLLodMeshShape now provides
  the first dedicated LoD-backed BoT mesh representation while preserving the
  existing SoBRLMeshShape full-field path for small or synchronous meshes, and
  LoD submission filters ordinary meshes unless explicitly configured
  otherwise.  BoT database realization now uses lodBotThreshold so the
  full-mesh path remains default and only threshold-matching BoTs become
  LoD-backed.  SoBRLMeshShape now has an explicit full-detail preservation
  policy: ordinary meshes preserve their pre-LoD indexed triangles when
  applying an active display-LoD payload, while SoBRLLodMeshShape opts out of
  that backup and keeps lightweight source counts/bounds for stable request
  identity after the full-detail fields are replaced.  For preserving meshes,
  SoBRLExportAction/SoBRLMeasureAction/SoBRLSnapAction can use that preserved
  source geometry for full-detail/detail-preserving policy requests while
  DISPLAY_LEVEL continues to use the displayed LoD mesh.  When no preserved
  full-detail payload is resident, exact export/measure/snap now record
  BRLObolSourceMeshRequest records carrying source identity, counts, and
  bounds instead of treating display LoD as exact geometry.  The first RT
  source full-detail provider can reload direct BoT source objects into owned
  Obol mesh payloads through BRLObolLodService, validates source-count
  mismatches as stale results, and exposes whole-object face/point limits so
  callers can refuse over-budget full-detail reloads before falling through to
  chunked detail reads.  BRLObolViewController now owns a per-view exact
  full-detail budget, and qtcad exact export/pick/snap/measure submit paths
  pass those limits to source-backed provider tasks.  Export, measure, and snap
  actions can now convert their recorded source requests into submit-ready RT
  full-detail LoD requests, and BRLObolLodService can submit those source
  requests with provider-owned cleanup.  Those actions can also consume
  returned full-detail mesh payloads using the recorded transform and
  shape-level metadata, and now expose action-level submit and drained-result
  consume helpers for recorded source-backed requests.  BRLObolViewController
  wraps those action-level paths for export, measure, snap, and source-backed
  mesh ray picking so higher-level callers can share filtered LoD-result drain,
  source-backed submission, and exact full-detail budget handling.  Exact mesh
  picking now has a matching
  source-backed traversal action that records full-detail source requests for
  LoD-backed meshes in exact-pick mode, can submit them through the LoD
  service, and can consume drained full-detail results via the shared
  pick-result ray tester.  qtcad point and rectangle picking now route exact
  mesh picks through the controller helper around provider drain points without
  blocking the UI, and qtcad's explicit full-detail snap, export, and geometry
  measure helpers now delegate source-backed LoD drain/submit work to
  BRLObolViewController while preserving source-backed face, vertex/edge,
  nearest-subentity, and edit-intent identity in their qtcad result records.
  Other
  exact-operation tools still need matching caller-side drain/submit
  integration.  The LoD service now supports request-filtered
  result draining, which lets
  source-backed exact-operation callers consume matching full-detail results
  without stealing unrelated display-LoD results from the shared queue.
  BRLObolViewController now resolves each source-backed exact request to the
  matching database source before submission, stamps controller-submitted
  full-detail requests with database/source revision identity, and has a
  multi-source test proving exact reloads do not fall through to the first
  database source in the view.  BRLObolLodService now exposes active-request
  lookup plus a unique-submit path, and the RT source full-detail submit
  helper uses it so repeated exact retries do not enqueue duplicate source
  reloads while an identical provider request is already queued or running.
  Controller exact helpers now also treat partially ready multi-request
  operations as a single pass: they consume ready source-backed payloads and
  submit the still-missing export/measure/snap or exact-pick requests before
  returning, avoiding an extra retry cycle per missing mesh.
  Measure, snap, and pick source-backed exact requests now carry source-local
  query metadata into LoD provider params: measure records nearest-query point
  bounds, snap records bounded query boxes and tolerance, and pick records
  source-local rays.  This gives the future chunked BoT reader the object
  identity and query scope needed to avoid whole-object reloads, while leaving
  the current whole-direct-BoT read path in place.  The direct BoT provider
  can trim returned face payloads and compact subset vertices for explicit
  bounded measure/snap requests carrying bounds plus tolerance and for
  exact-pick requests carrying a source-local ray, and it now applies exact
  full-detail face/point budgets to those scoped payloads after reduction.
  Only `source_query.space=source_local`
  enables direct BoT payload reduction or scoped budget bypass; mixed-kind,
  duplicate, malformed, or non-source-local query metadata stays whole-object
  for correctness.  Scoped bounds/ray misses now return a terminal no-result fallback instead of
  silently broadening to a whole-object direct BoT payload.  Those subset
  payloads now include optional source face- and vertex-index maps so export,
  measure, snap, and pick consumers preserve the original BoT ids instead of
  reporting packed subset slots.  The source-request-to-LoD conversion helper
  now strips stale `source_query.*` params from template requests before
  appending the current source-backed query metadata, so request identity and
  provider execution do not drift through duplicate or mixed template params.
  Pick
  consumers accept partial source
  payloads only for ray-scoped requests; non-query full-detail results still
  require whole-object face counts.  Unbounded measure hint requests
  deliberately stay whole-object because global nearest-primitive measurement
  needs a complete search domain.  Measure now has an explicit query distance
  limit for bounded-neighborhood semantics; with that opt-in, source-backed
  measure requests carry bounds plus tolerance, resident measurement filters
  nearest candidates by the same distance, the direct BoT provider may reduce
  returned faces after scoped bounds selection, and the measure consumer
  accepts compact source subsets only when source face and compact vertex maps
  are present.
  The LoD submit-action gate now also pins non-forced view-policy active-level
  scheduling by comparing a submitted provider result with the level selected
  directly by `rt_mesh_lod_load_view` for the same `rt_view_info`; forced-level
  overrides remain covered separately.  The submit action also skips
  duplicate provider work when a ready mesh LoD result for the same
  view/policy/provider request key is already resident or already
  queued/running in the LoD service, while reset and forced-level submissions
  still bypass that no-op path.
  Mesh ray
  picking now has the same initial policy split: display-level is the viewport
  default, while explicit full-detail picks use resident preserved source
  triangles when available and otherwise refuse to treat an active display-LoD
  payload as exact geometry.  That preservation is an interim correctness
  bridge for meshes that are already resident, not the target policy for large
  geometry.
  Production LoD-backed BoTs must be
  source-backed and memory-budgeted, keeping identity/bounds/active display
  payloads resident while exact operations fetch full-detail or bounded chunks
  from the .g/RT provider path on demand.  Exact picks for CSG implicit solids
  and implicit-solid-backed comb instances should use a librt ray query rather
  than tessellated display geometry.  libbrlobol now has the first direct
  librt ray helper for explicit database object paths; it returns the nearest
  implicit/comb hit with point, normal, distance, region/material data, source
  primitive name/type, and BRL-CAD pick-detail identity.  qtcad point,
  explicit ray, and rectangle picks now call that helper for database-source
  implicit/comb fallback hits, including camera-derived rays for point-pick
  scenes that have database sources but no display-pickable Obol geometry.
  BRLObolViewController owns reusable RT exact-pick caches for its current
  database sources, keeps them across repeated point, ray, and rectangle pick
  calls when the source signature is unchanged, and clears or rebuilds them
  when scene/source ownership or source revisions change.  qtcad point and
  ray picks now merge librt exact candidates into the same distance-ordered
  record set as display and source-backed mesh picks while suppressing
  same-path RT/display duplicates, and rectangle picks inherit that ordering
  through sampled point picks.  QgSelectRayFilter now tries the Obol
  explicit-ray helper before its legacy BSG/RT fallback, and that Obol ray
  selection path can run without the filter's legacy `dbip` field.
  POP payload ownership, active-level selection, action/pick consumption of
  source-backed full-detail reloads, chunked detail reads from the recorded
  source requests when no preserved payload is resident,
  optional coarse POP-build spatial/index sidecar metadata for targeted exact
  picks/snaps, remaining non-qtcad tool integration of the librt exact-pick
  helper, applying the merged ordering policy to those exact-pick tools, and
  richer policy-aware provider scheduling are still queued.
* libbrlobol shaded PNTS realization now preserves all RT PNTS point-record
  variants as Obol vlist point primitives rather than failing the shaded path
  or forcing a mesh.  SoBRLVListShape exposes point primitive inspection plus
  optional per-point color, scale, and normal accessors; scale contributes to
  scene/export bounds, SoBRLExportAction exports point records with source and
  attribute identity with transform-aware point normals/scale bounds, snap
  treats point primitives as endpoint candidates, and prototype tests cover
  shaded PNTS realization, bbox, pick detail, export, snap behavior, every
  intermediate color/scale/normal attribute layout, global scale fallback,
  per-point scale precedence, and transformed attribute export.  A native
  point/marker node remains deferred.
* libbrlobol shaded sketch and annotation realization now uses the same RT
  plot-backed SoBRLVListShape geometry as wire mode for those curve-like
  non-surface primitives.  This keeps annotations and 2-D sketch curves
  drawable, pickable, snappable, exportable, and measurable in shaded scenes
  without pretending they are tessellated surface meshes; native Obol text and
  curve nodes remain queued.
* libbrlobol standalone material database realization now has a stable Obol
  representation.  SoBRLMaterialObject stores database path/source identity,
  material name, parent/source strings, and grouped property key/value pairs
  without contributing drawable geometry.  Prototype tests cover wire and
  shaded material-object realization plus no-draw bbox, pick, snap, export,
  and measure behavior.
* qtcad still has a transitional BSG-to-Obol camera bridge.  The bridge is
  correctly kept in libqtcad, not libbrlobol.
* qtcad's visible rendering path is still transitional.  Obol render/readback
  works and GL/SW migrated-content paints bypass libdm, but libdm/BSG are
  still part of current application rendering for non-migrated content and
  legacy fallback paths.

Dependency guardrails:

* brlobol_pivot_guard is active as a transitional CTest guard.
* The guard blocks new BSG/DM leakage in Obol-canonical code and rejects
  direct BSG vlist/vlblock symbols in libbrlobol.
* doc/notes/obol_legacy_dependency_inventory.txt records the phase-owned
  direct BSG/DM dependency rows for the Obol pivot, and the guard checks the
  exact rows against src/source_dirs.cmake.
* The guard checks that qtcad Obol workflow tests do not directly link libdm,
  libbsg, or dm_plugins while they exercise the transitional application path.
* The guard rejects reintroducing BSG measurement feature/result fallback
  symbols or the old BSG-pick/raytrace 3D measurement fallback in
  QgMeasureFilter while the rest of qtcad continues its staged migration.
* The guard rejects reintroducing public BSG/raytrace selection accessors in
  QgSelectFilter and direct BSG interaction consumption in the qged selector
  plugin.
* The guard checks that the default plot3 implementation is owned by the
  normal libbg implementation unit and is not reintroduced through libbsg or
  standalone bg test executables.  It also checks that bg_line_layer keeps
  its explicit plot3 diagnostic export helpers and that standalone plot3
  utilities do not relink libbsg.
* The framebuffer/image-stream plan records the split: libimgstream owns
  non-display stream storage, transport, producer/subscriber lifecycle, and
  fb compatibility; libicv owns static images/codecs/lookup tables; Obol and
  libbrlobol own image display and window-host behavior.
* The initial libimgstream target now provides an opaque imgstream_* C API for
  RGB/RGBA memory streams, resize, clear, full-frame and rectangle
  read/write, stream info/metadata snapshots, dirty-region generation,
  subscriber notification, producer begin/end state, and icv_image_t snapshot
  conversion without linking libdm, libbsg, or libbrlobol.
* libbrlobol now provides SoBRLImageSource as the first Obol image-stream
  source ownership node.  It can wrap static icv_image_t data or borrowed
  libimgstream streams, tracks stream metadata/revisions in Obol fields, and
  subscribes to dirty-region notifications.  SoBRLViewportImage and
  SoBRLImagePlane are the first display consumers: they read source streams,
  rebuild standard Obol texture quads when source data/dirty revisions change,
  and expose the viewport/image-plane policy fields needed by framebuffer
  display migration.  Incremental backend dirty-region texture uploads and
  real window-host integration remain queued.
* The first libimgstream framebuffer compatibility unit is in place as
  imgstream/fb_compat.h and src/libimgstream/fb_compat.c.  It maps memory
  framebuffer requests to RGB imgstream buffers, preserves legacy view,
  window, zoom, cursor, screen-cursor, and cursor-shape compatibility
  spellings plus reset, viewport, poll, and poll-rate compatibility as
  non-display state/operations, supports RGB and BW rectangle compatibility,
  and supports raw RGB file sink/source compatibility for
  ordinary file paths plus explicit /dev/disk:<path> or /dev/disk=<path>
  spelling.  It also supports /dev/stack fan-out to memory/file child sinks
  and /dev/null, /dev/debug, and /dev/txt diagnostic sinks without a display
  backend.  It preserves legacy 256-entry RGB colormap state through a neutral
  imgstream_fb_colormap type and rmap/wmap compatibility calls.  It classifies
  and normalizes remote/display specifiers through imgstream_fb_spec_info,
  including numeric port specs, host:port, host:port:device, tcp:, ipc:, and
  display framebuffer names.  Display-capable framebuffer opens now create an
  RGB stream and delegate open/close, view, cursor, viewport, flush, reset,
  and poll behavior to a registered toolkit-neutral display host callback;
  without that callback they still fail explicitly until the Obol/libbrlobol
  display-host migration connects real windows.
* The guard still needs to grow as more directories become Obol-canonical.

Low-level neutralization:

* include/bg/vlist.h owns bg_vlist, bg_vlblock, BG_VLIST_* commands, helper
  macros, and pure helper declarations.
* src/libbg/vlist.c owns the pure vlist helper implementations, including
  command counting, bbox, validation, copy/import/export/cleanup, vlblock
  helpers, RPP emission, ARB8 wire emission, text stroking, and plot3/uplot
  export.
* BG-owned polygon and SAT interfaces no longer include BSG headers.  The
  pivot guard prevents include/bg and src/libbg from reintroducing direct BSG
  header dependencies.
* include/rt/vlist.h exposes RT-facing aliases over bg_vlist.  The RT-named
  text/uplot helpers are wrappers over the BG-owned implementation.
* include/bsg/vlist.h, src/libbsg/vlist.c, and src/libbsg/font.c are
  compatibility layers.  They should disappear with libbsg.
* Most librt primitive wire emitters, the NMG edit-test helper, prep plot
  export, annotation text stroking, and librt text/uplot bridges now include
  rt/vlist.h and use RT_VLIST_* or RT-named helper APIs instead of BSG vlist
  names.  The pivot guard prevents those callers from regressing to
  bsg/vlist.h, bsg_vlist/bsg_vlblock, or BSG_*VLIST macros.  The BREP debug
  vlblock declaration also uses rt_vlblock, so src/librt no longer has direct
  bsg/vlist.h users.
* librt primitive wire/LoD line emitters use RT_PRIMITIVE_* line-command names
  for move, draw, and point records instead of BSG_GEOMETRY_* names.  The
  command encodings intentionally match BG line-layer and legacy BSG streams,
  and the pivot guard prevents src/librt/primitives from regressing to
  bsg/geometry.h or BSG_GEOMETRY_* names.
* include/rt and non-test src/librt no longer include the broad bsg.h umbrella
  header.  Remaining transitional BSG use in librt is routed through specific
  headers and explicit legacy adapters while BSG-backed compatibility paths are
  retired.  The pivot guard prevents reintroducing the BSG
  umbrella include.
* include/rt/edit.h owns rt_edit_knobs plus its reset/hash helpers and no
  longer includes bsg/defines.h or embeds bsg_view_knobs.  It also owns the
  rt_edit_view snapshot used by rt_edit runtime state, so struct rt_edit no
  longer stores a BSG view pointer.  The main edit create/reinit/knob APIs now
  accept rt_edit_view; callers that still own BSG view state opt into the
  transitional include/rt/edit_legacy_bsg.h adapters.  The neutral
  src/librt/edit.cpp implementation is free of BSG headers and adapter logic;
  src/librt/edit_legacy_bsg.cpp owns the BSG view-state adapter
  implementation.  The src/librt/tests/edit primitive edit tests now
  construct RT-owned rt_edit_view snapshots directly through a local test
  helper instead of creating BSG views.
* include/rt/view.h owns the neutral rt_view_info, rt_view_lod_settings, and
  rt_view_lod_policy snapshots used by RT primitive plot, LoD realization,
  indexed-face-set callbacks, and transitional view LoD policy adapters, plus
  RT_VIEW_* normalized view-coordinate constants and RT_VIEW_MIN_SIZE /
  RT_VIEW_MIN_SCALE limits for callers that only need historical view numeric
  policy.  rt_view_info_sanitize and rt_view_lod_policy_sanitize normalize
  invalid dimensions, view size, and nonpositive LoD scales to neutral defaults
  at adapter boundaries.
  include/rt/functab.h and the primitive callback declarations no longer
  expose bsg_view for those callbacks; BSG-backed callers adapt through the
  transitional include/rt/view_legacy_bsg.h boundary.
  src/librt/view.c contains only the neutral RT view helper implementation and
  exposes RT-prefixed view metric helpers for LoD curve scale, BoT threshold,
  average sample spacing, and solid point spacing; primitive LoD code uses
  those RT names instead of private primitive/view helper declarations.
  src/librt/view_legacy_bsg.c owns the BSG view, LoD policy get/set/copy,
  LoD bounds-update/callback, view-scale/size/inverse-size, keypoint,
  rotate-about, coord, and scene-ref LoD load/free adapter implementation.  BSG
  mesh-LoD unwrapping is private to librt implementation units rather than an
  installed legacy adapter surface.
  src/libged's private primitive wireframe and face-set publication helpers
  now consume rt_view_info snapshots before invoking RT primitive callbacks;
  BSG-to-RT view adaptation is centralized in the transitional
  src/libged/bsg_ged_draw_view.c helper used by the BSG-owning caller
  wrappers, and those wrappers now receive the adapter declaration through the
  private bsg_ged_draw_private.h boundary rather than one-off declarations.
  The same helper wraps the RT-owned LoD policy snapshot for draw.cpp, draw
  refs, transaction redraw paths, and the draw command's temporary
  BoT-threshold mutation/restore compatibility path, plus the transitional BSG
  LoD bounds-update hook used by transaction redraw.  The libged lod and view

  get/set, BSG view bounds-callback identity, view-scale extraction, and

  implementation.
  libged plot, vnirt, solids_on_ray, ADC, and DM window-bound

* include/rt/view.h exposes mesh LoD cache lookups through an opaque
  rt_mesh_lod handle instead of returning bsg_mesh_lod directly.  The current
  cache implementation remains BSG-backed internally, but RT now exposes
  neutral rt_mesh_lod_load_level, rt_mesh_lod_load_view, and
  rt_mesh_lod_data_get helpers for loading and inspecting cached mesh data
  without exposing the BSG container.  rt_mesh_lod_current_level reports the
  active level without changing residency, and rt_mesh_lod_memshrink releases
  active CPU-side POP/full-detail arrays while preserving the current level so
  callers can reload on demand.  rt_mesh_lod_info_get provides a copied
  RT-owned active-level, count, bounds, snapped-point, and normal summary for
  staged realization decisions that should not borrow provider arrays.
  db_mesh_lod_status, db_mesh_lod_refresh, and db_mesh_lod_invalidate expose
  neutral cache-key, cached-payload, stale-entry, generated, and cleared-entry
  reporting for staged realization and invalidation logic; libged database
  index invalidation now uses the RT-owned invalidation helper rather than
  direct BSG mesh-LoD key/cache mutation.  db_mesh_lod_clear handles
  per-database cache clearing and rt_mesh_lod_cache_clear_all owns the explicit
  global cache-clear operation, so libged view lod cache-clear commands no
  longer call BSG cache clearing directly.  The same view lod cache command
  now refreshes BoT cache entries through db_mesh_lod_refresh and checks cache
  presence through db_mesh_lod_status.  The BSG-backed draw.cpp BoT mesh LoD
  realization path now uses db_mesh_lod_status/refresh/get for cache
  ownership, stores the RT-owned rt_mesh_lod handle in the draw source state,
  publishes active mesh geometry through the copied RT mesh-LoD data view, and
  passes that RT handle to private GED adapters that own the remaining
  transitional BSG scene-ref visibility/load unwrapping.  db_mesh_lod_store_mesh now
  stores generated mesh payloads through the same RT cache facade, so libged
  view lod and draw.cpp BRep paths no longer write BSG mesh-LoD cache keys directly;
  BRep tessellation itself remains a transitional libged-side producer until
  RT owns BRep mesh-LoD generation.
  Legacy callers no longer get an installed BSG mesh-LoD extraction helper;
  BSG unwrapping stays private to librt implementation units, and transitional
  BSG scene-ref loading is exposed only through narrow RT legacy helper calls.
  librt private database state owns an opaque rt_mesh_lod_context rather than
  bsg_mesh_lod_context; the RT cache path self-initializes that private context
  and only the cache implementation unwraps the current BSG backing context.
  libged draw_data_t and ged_draw_source_state no longer carry the old
  bsg_mesh_lod_context pass-through, and ged_draw_source_state no longer stores
  a borrowed bsg_mesh_lod pointer; draw realization asks RT cache helpers
  for LoD state when it needs it.  Public struct ged no longer exposes or
  manually creates/destroys a BSG mesh-LoD context; GED draw tests invalidate
  LoD cache entries through the RT cache facade.  BSG draw helper units that
  do not own transitional mesh-LoD payload operations no longer include
  bsg/lod.h, and draw.cpp no longer includes bsg/lod.h directly.  The private
  libged BSG-to-RT view helper no longer includes bsg/lod.h or names direct
  BSG bounds/scene-ref LoD APIs.  BRep full-detail callback registration now
  uses RT mesh-LoD detail callbacks, so src/libged no longer includes
  bsg/lod.h for LoD publication.
  src/librt/tests/lod.c now validates cached mesh-LoD levels through the
  RT-owned helpers instead of creating BSG views, mesh nodes, or LoD contexts.
* The former POP, LoD pipeline, LoD wakeup notes, and cache_drawing.cpp
  define the LoD rework direction for Obol/libbrlobol.  LoD realization
  should be a provider-neutral staged service below Obol, not a BSG LoD node
  port.  Obol/libbrlobol should
  request immutable realized geometry for database path, database identity,
  source revision or content hash, view state, draw mode, LoD policy, provider
  id/version, and provider parameters, then apply staged results on the
  UI/main thread only when source/view/policy generation tokens still match.
  The staged result sequence should be: metadata/hierarchy, attributes plus
  AABB, OBB or other cheap proxy, fast display LoD such as POP Buffers,
  qualified high-quality/progressive mesh LoD for clean meshes, and
  exact/full-detail fallback for editing, exact picking, export, or
  still-frame refinement.
  The old cache_drawing.cpp experiment contributes useful production concepts:
  fast-to-slow preparation, main-thread result draining, one serialized
  bu_cache/LMDB write lane, debug delay knobs for observing intermediate
  states, explicit AABB/OBB/LoD milestones, and queue-based handoff between
  independent workers.  Do not carry forward its BSG-specific API,
  object-name-only cache keys, detached unmanaged workers, void* lifetime
  pattern, fixed linear pipeline assumption, or rt_db_internal ownership by
  comment.  Use an owned task service with explicit start/stop, joinable
  shutdown, cancellation tokens, generation ids, explicit in-flight counts,
  task-graph dependencies, and clear final ownership of cracked internals.
  concurrentqueue.h may be used for multi-producer/multi-consumer stage and
  result handoff, but size_approx() must remain diagnostic, idle-wait, or
  settled-check only.  Correctness must come from enqueue/dequeue success,
  shutdown/cancellation tokens, generation ids, and in-flight counters.
  Result records should carry object path/name, source revision, view/policy
  revision, result kind, quality tier, provider id, cache key or geometry
  handle, bounds, primitive counts, estimated error when known,
  terminal/fallback status, and diagnostics.
  The LoD wakeup design adds the required wakeup contract: BRLObolLodService
  should emit a lightweight, coalesced result-ready callback when the result
  queue transitions from empty to non-empty; callbacks must not mutate Obol
  nodes or deliver results; the owning controller sets a pending flag, wakes
  or is observed by the main loop, and drains/applies results on the UI/main
  thread with bounded-drain support.  This uses the existing mutex/condition
  service queues for now; concurrentqueue.h remains an option for later
  high-volume inter-stage/result handoff once the public contract is stable.
  The concrete Obol/libbrlobol build-out should start by closing the neutral
  RT facade gaps that libbrlobol will need: current active level/status,
  active-data residency status, memory-shrink/reload hooks, cache
  invalidation or stale-entry status, and copied metadata that avoids
  borrowing provider arrays.  The initial
  provider-neutral BRLObolLodRequest, BRLObolLodResult, cache-key, and
  geometry-handle contract is in place, including deterministic cache keys
  over database/source/view/policy/provider identity, bounds, counts, and
  provider parameters.  The initial owned BRLObolLodService now provides
  start/stop, joinable workers, generation cancellation, task dependencies,
  main-thread result draining, in-flight diagnostics, cancellable task-level
  debug delay controls, stale-result rejection, owned realization-task data
  cleanup, a coalesced result-ready subscription/wakeup, and a serialized
  cache-write lane.  Typed Stage 0/1/2 result payloads and
  constructors now cover dependency/directory records, display attributes,
  AABB, and proxy/OBB milestones.  The first RT mesh-cache provider adapter
  can run as a service task, refresh missing cache entries, load the active
  RT mesh-LoD view level, copy the active RT mesh-LoD arrays into an owned
  staged mesh payload, and publish the neutral mesh result without exposing
  BSG.  Next wire POP/provider payload ownership and a dedicated LoD-backed
  Obol node/update pass while the existing direct mesh/vlist realization
  remains available for small and synchronous cases.  The first
  SoBRLLodUpdateAction already handles UI-thread result drains and applies
  staged metadata/attribute/proxy records plus owned mesh payloads to matching
  SoBRLMeshShape nodes.  Mesh nodes can now build deterministic staged LoD
  requests from their Obol identity fields, and the view controller can expose
  a sanitized rt_view_info snapshot.  The first explicit
  SoBRLMeshLodSubmitAction uses those pieces to submit RT mesh-cache provider
  tasks for matching mesh nodes, and BRLObolViewController now has explicit
  submit/drain/apply methods plus a retained LoD service subscription and
  pending-result processing path that wire the action pair to the active
  scene, view snapshot, service, and database source list.  It also has the
  first auto-submit gate, keyed by view, policy, and source signature, for
  render-time LoD request preparation; that source signature includes
  lodBotThreshold policy input.  The remaining controller/render integration
  work is to make scheduling richer and provider/policy-aware.
  Large BoTs
  should realize through a dedicated
  LoD-backed mesh node, preferably SoBRLLodMeshShape, or a clearly separated
  LoD-backed mode on SoBRLMeshShape.  That node should store persistent
  source/material/selection/LoD policy fields in Obol while keeping large
  active POP arrays private, reloadable, and governed by RT-owned
  rt_mesh_lod handles.  A narrow SoBRLMeshLodUpdateAction or extension of the
  current action/controller pass should build rt_view_info from the Obol camera
  and viewport, call the
  neutral rt_mesh_lod_load_view/data APIs, update backend/private geometry
  only when the active level changes or a reload is requested, and mark the
  node dirty through normal Obol notification.  Actions must distinguish
  display-geometry behavior from exact/model behavior: viewport pick may use
  active display LoD, while precision measure, edit snap, and default export
  need an explicit full-detail or detail-preserving policy.  Export, measure,
  and snap now expose that policy split; full-detail reloads and pick policy
  wiring remain.
* include/rt/primitives/sketch.h exposes sketch-to-polygon and
  polygon-to-sketch conversion through an opaque rt_sketch_polygon handle
  instead of bsg_polygon_ref.  The current BSG view-polygon workflow opts into
  include/rt/primitives/sketch_legacy_bsg.h for the old
  db_sketch_to_view_polygon* and db_view_polygon_ref_to_sketch adapters.
  src/librt/primitives/sketch/polygons.c now stores the opaque handle in
  RT-owned polygon state and is free of BSG headers; BSG polygon-ref
  translation is limited to
  src/librt/primitives/sketch/polygons_legacy_bsg.c.
* src/libnmg now includes bg/vlist.h directly, uses bg_vlist/bg_vlblock and
  BG_VLIST_* helper APIs for NMG plot/debug output, and no longer links
  libbsg directly.  The pivot guard prevents these NMG files from regressing
  to BSG vlist names.
* libanalyze's NIRT segment plotting now includes bg/vlist.h directly and uses
  BG vlist/vlblock helpers.  libanalyze records a direct libbg dependency for
  that use, and the pivot guard prevents NIRT from regressing to BSG vlist
  names.
* dxf-g, IGES drawing import, g-jack, g-off, ganalyze, and tea_nmg use
  bg/vlist.h directly for their low-level vlist/uplot work instead of
  bsg/vlist.h compatibility aliases.  The pivot guard prevents those utility
  paths from regressing to BSG vlist names.
* src/libbrep uses BG-owned vlist/plot helpers for debug plot output and no
  longer records a direct libbsg dependency.  Any remaining BREP BSG
  publication is in GED/librt transitional scene paths, not libbrep.  The
  pivot guard prevents src/libbrep and include/brep from regressing to BSG
  names.
* include/bg/line_layer.h and src/libbg/line_layer.c own the neutral
  color-keyed line-layer builder and the simple bg_line_layer-to-plot3 stream
  export helpers used for legacy diagnostic output.
* BREP, NMG, GQA, and check diagnostic paths now build bg_line_layer data and
  cross back into BSG only at the temporary publication boundary.
* include/bg/plot3.h is the canonical installed plot3 header.
  include/bsg/plot3.h is a transitional wrapper.
* Standalone plot3 utilities now link through libbg/libbu as appropriate,
  not libbsg.  plot3-dm remains a legacy display/Tk utility path.
* The old BSG TIG and vectfont headers are no longer part of the public BSG
  header set after the newdraw rebase.
* The default plot3_* implementation now lives in a normal libbg
  implementation unit.  The PLOT3_IMPLEMENTATION header block remains only
  for private prefixed local implementations; plot3.h is expected to remain a
  useful debugging/export interface.


What We Should Not Rebuild
--------------------------

Do not recreate BSG inside libbrlobol.

The earlier BSG direction was useful because it separated application drawing
state from individual display backends.  Obol already provides the stronger
version of that model: a real scene graph, traversal/actions, details,
render managers, text, cameras, and pluggable backends.  The cleaner long
term design is to put BRL-CAD-specific behavior into Obol extensions and let
applications use Obol features directly.

libbrlobol may provide narrow controllers and services where BRL-CAD needs
coordination, such as database realization, diagnostics, edit preview, and
application render scheduling.  Those services should hold Obol objects and
BRL-CAD policy, not generic render lists, payload stores, or backend-neutral
scene abstractions parallel to Obol.


Target Public API Shape
-----------------------

libbrlobol:

* brlobol_init initializes Obol and BRL-CAD Obol classes.
* Database source nodes own BRL-CAD database/path realization state as
  normal Obol node fields.
* BRLObolViewController coordinates scene root, camera, SoViewport,
  SoRenderManager, render scheduling, readback, realization, and diagnostics.
* Realization, export, snap, measure, pick, diagnostics, edit preview, HUD,
  overlays, and text are expressed as Obol nodes/actions/details.
* SoBRLSceneController remains a narrow realization/diagnostic service.
* No public bsg_render_item, bsg_backend_scene, generic payload store, or
  BSG-like render list appears in libbrlobol.

qtcad/qged:

* Migrated code uses BRLObolViewController and Obol scene/camera objects.
* Existing bsg_view, dm, and fb accessors remain only during parallel
  migration.
* Final public drawing APIs do not expose bsg_view, dm, fb, or dm_plugins.
* vlist is not exposed as an application scene abstraction.  Wire output is
  converted to Obol line/shape nodes at the scene boundary.

GED/TclCAD/mged:

* GED drawing services update Obol database source nodes, Obol camera/view
  state, and Obol selection/pick details.
* C/Tcl callers use an opaque bridge backed by Obol where needed.
* No public BSG/DM compatibility shim survives final removal.

Image streams, framebuffer compatibility, and window hosting:

* libimgstream provides opaque imgstream_* stream lifecycle, metadata, pixel
  read/write, rect update, snapshot, subscriber, producer, and transport APIs.
* libimgstream/fb_compat preserves the current framebuffer API/protocol
  spellings needed by rt, remrt, fbserv, GED/TclCAD, and src/fb tools while
  translating them to image-stream storage, transport, or explicit
  unsupported diagnostics.
* SoBRLImageSource represents static icv_image_t data and live libimgstream
  streams as Obol data sources.
* SoBRLViewportImage displays an image source in viewport-aligned underlay,
  overlay, HUD, render-progress, and framebuffer-compatibility roles.
* SoBRLImagePlane displays an image source as a textured scene-space plane.
* libbrlobol owns the toolkit-neutral BRLObolWindowHost contract around
  BRLObolViewController, normalized input events, semantic input profiles,
  and display-host callbacks used by display-capable fb_open and dm_open
  compatibility.  The neutral contract and initial imgstream display-host
  bridge are in place; display-capable framebuffer streams can attach to
  SoBRLImageSource and SoBRLViewportImage nodes under the controller.
  BRLObolHeadlessWindowHost provides the first offscreen adapter and drains
  those streams through Obol readback; qtcad's QgObolWindowHost provides the
  first Qt canvas adapter for the same display-host contract and can create an
  owned visible Qt toplevel QgSW fallback when no qtcad canvas exists.  General
  dm_open routing, final Qt host selection, and Tk/MGED adapters remain.
* libimgstream must not depend on libbrlobol.  Display-capable framebuffer
  compatibility obtains or creates an image stream, then delegates window or
  viewport display through a registered libbrlobol/application display host.


Progress Snapshot
-----------------

These are approximate completion estimates for the current checked-in path.
They measure exit-criteria completion, not implementation effort spent.

Next Push:

* Obol realization coverage matrix: 98 percent.  The matrix and required
  pinewood.g, havoc.g, and m35.g gates exist, and Generic_Twin.g is documented
  as the larger FAA maturity candidate with an opt-in
  BRLOBOL_QTCAD_GENERIC_TWIN=1 draw/readback hook for the final mixed
  implicit/BoT gate; standalone edge entity picking, standalone vertex entity
  picking, and broader live edit-tool lifecycle semantics remain.  Mesh face
  picks and triangle export records now preserve source vertex indices for
  face-level inspection/export, and face pick details record
  nearest edge and vertex identity hints.  Preview-level edit-intent identity
  can now be distinct from preview-node identity and propagates through the
  action/export paths.  PNTS point primitives now have shaded Obol realization
  coverage for all RT point-record layouts.  The required pinewood.g/havoc.g
  prototype hierarchy checks now use export and measure action summary counts
  instead of recursive random-access `getRealizedShape/Mesh(i)` counting, and
  the count-only wire hierarchy measure now opts out of connected-angle
  searching while preserving the default measurement behavior elsewhere.
  Default wire measurement now enumerates connected-angle candidates with
  endpoint buckets, so sparse line sets avoid the previous all-pairs segment
  scan while keeping the same shared-vertex tolerance and nearest-angle
  selection path.  The remaining runtime was havoc.g's shaded realization,
  which duplicated shaded hierarchy coverage already retained by pinewood.g;
  havoc.g now stays as the larger wire hierarchy traversal.  This reduces the
  FreeBSD/clang prototype test from roughly 107 seconds to roughly 1.4 seconds
  without weakening the hierarchy coverage.  A later timing pass found the
  remaining large hierarchy measurement cost in SoBRLMeasureAction's indexed
  vlist access pattern: getSegment(i) rescanned the SoBRLVListShape command
  stream for every segment.  Measurement and snap action traversal now walk
  the command stream once, keeping the same segment ordinals, point primitive
  indices, filters, transforms, bounds, nearest results, and angle behavior.
  On this FreeBSD/clang build, pinewood.g wire measure fell from about 0.279
  seconds to about 0.003 seconds, havoc.g wire measure fell from about 0.159
  seconds to about 0.049 seconds, and libbrlobol_prototype CTest fell from
  about 1.44 seconds to about 0.96 seconds.
* libbrlobol database realization: 98 percent.  Real hierarchy realization,
  partial refresh, identity fields, mesh/vlist representation, and tests are
  in place; mesh face pick/export details carry source vertex indices and face
  picks expose nearest edge/vertex identity hints; BoT meshes can publish
  cached RT LoD metadata into Obol fields and export records through the
  initial provider-neutral LoD result contract, and the first async
  BRLObolLodService skeleton plus typed Stage 0/1/2 dependency, attribute,
  AABB, and proxy payloads are available for staged work.  The first RT
  mesh-cache provider task can refresh/load cached RT mesh LoD through the
  async service, force a requested RT LoD level, shrink active RT arrays after
  copying, and return a neutral staged mesh result with an owned Obol-side
  triangle payload; explicit database invalidation is now covered at the
  provider boundary, with no-refresh providers surfacing cache misses and
  refresh-enabled providers rebuilding the RT cache before returning an Obol
  payload.  SoBRLMeshShape can
  now consume staged results into Obol fields and reject stale, cancelled,
  error, and cache-miss result statuses, and SoBRLLodUpdateAction can drain
  service results and apply accepted staged records and owned mesh payloads to
  matching scene nodes;
  request/view helper boundaries now exist, and the first explicit
  SoBRLMeshLodSubmitAction can use them to submit RT mesh-cache provider work
  from an Obol scene and view snapshot; BRLObolViewController now exposes
  explicit submit/drain/apply LoD methods with counters and diagnostics for
  application/controller integration, and the service/controller wakeup path
  can mark queued results pending, process them on the main thread, and
  request a render after applied staged results; the controller can now
  coalesce automatic LoD submissions by view/policy/source signature so
  render-time preparation avoids duplicate requests for an unchanged scene and
  lodBotThreshold policy changes are part of that source signature; it also
  tracks a sanitized RT view signature so direct camera field changes can
  trigger a fresh LoD submission without replacing the camera node, and
  forced RT LoD levels can be requested through both SoBRLMeshLodSubmitAction
  and BRLObolViewController as an explicit policy input;
  SoBRLLodMeshShape now provides the first dedicated LoD-backed BoT node
  identity, BoT realization uses it only when lodBotThreshold is positive and
  the BoT face count meets/exceeds the threshold while currently loading
  full-field mesh data for first realization, and LoD submission skips
  ordinary meshes unless explicitly configured otherwise; dedicated
  LoD-backed mesh nodes now disable full-detail backup when display-LoD
  payloads replace active fields, while preserving lightweight source
  counts/bounds for request identity.  Ordinary meshes can still preserve
  full-detail indexed triangles as an interim resident-mesh fallback.  Mesh
  nodes can now evict both preserved full-detail arrays and active
  display-LoD payloads while retaining source-backed request identity, source
  counts, bounds, exact-operation provider routing, and bbox contribution.
  SoBRLMeshResidencyAction and BRLObolViewController's explicit and optional
  controller-owned mesh-budget paths can now enforce a resident mesh byte
  budget across a scene by evicting preserved full detail first and
  source-backed active display payloads second, including automatic
  enforcement after LoD results are applied.  Measure, snap, and pick exact
  source requests now carry source-local bounded-query metadata into LoD
  provider params, so the partial-read provider can distinguish whole-object
  exact reloads from nearest-point, bbox, or ray-scoped source queries.  The
  direct BoT provider now uses snap bounds/tolerance and exact-pick rays to
  reduce returned face payloads, compact subset vertices, preserve source
  face/vertex ids, and honor face/point budgets after source-local scoped
  snap/pick reduction without broadening scoped misses to whole-object
  payloads or trusting mixed/ambiguous/malformed/non-source-local query
  metadata while keeping measure hint requests whole-object for correctness.
  The production large-BoT path still needs source-backed full-detail access
  that can fetch bbox/intersection-targeted subsets or chunk very large meshes
  instead of requiring a full resident copy.  A candidate
  exact-query optimization is a cached coarse spatial/index sidecar emitted
  while the POP build is already walking source triangles: assign triangles to
  normalized Morton/grid leaves, union real triangle bboxes into leaf bounds,
  and store source face ids or ranges for provider subset queries.  Keep it
  optional and coarse; it becomes an out-of-core win only when paired with a
  librt BoT partial-read or cached full-detail chunk API.  Export and
  measure now have explicit full-detail versus display-LoD policies that use
  preserved full-detail source geometry when available, snap defaults to
  display-level behavior with an explicit full-detail detail-preserving mode
  that can also use preserved source geometry, and mesh ray picking has an
  explicit display/default versus preserved-full-detail policy split;
  libbrlobol has the first reusable BRLObolRtPickCache for exact CSG implicit
  and comb-instance hits, preparing explicit database object paths once and
  shooting repeated rays through that RT instance while the existing one-shot
  helper remains as a wrapper.  It returns path/source identity,
  region/material data, hit point, normal, and distance.  BRLObolViewController
  owns the interactive cache lifetime/invalidation path and now exposes both
  a nonblocking source-backed mesh exact ray helper and a cache-backed exact
  RT ray helper for non-qtcad callers; qtcad point, explicit ray, and
  rectangle picks delegate to those shared helpers before applying their
  display/source duplicate suppression.  Broader non-qtcad exact-pick tool
  integration remains.
  qtcad rectangle picks already reuse that cache across sampled
  implicit/comb exact-pick rays; PNTS shaded point
  realization/export/snap is covered, including all RT point-record layouts
  with optional color, scale, and normal attributes on the fallback point
  shape plus global-scale fallback, per-point scale precedence, and
  transform-aware attribute export; sketch and annotation shaded realization
  now use plot-backed vlist geometry with pick/snap/export/measure coverage
  for curve-like non-surface primitives; standalone material database objects
  now realize as no-draw SoBRLMaterialObject metadata nodes;
  native text/curve/material integration refinements, standalone edge and
  vertex entity picking, POP/provider payload ownership, richer provider-level
  active-level policy scheduling, and more shaded converter hardening remain.
  Controller-owned database-source replacement now configures watched source
  fields atomically so realization is not invalidated by stale delayed field
  sensors immediately after qtcad sync.
* Obol diagnostic overlays: 45 percent.  qtcad Obol line-layer/HUD overlay
